That makes him a plausible fit, but you need to show what Papercusp adds. [His team’s current projects](https://github.com/Intelligent-Internet).\n\n1. **Record a 90-second demo.** Give agents a useful task, interrupt a session, resume it with decisions intact, and show the finished result and its verification. Pick something reproducible.\n2. **Connect it to his ownership thesis.** Intelligent Internet emphasizes AI people own and control. Show concretely how Papercusp supports that through a workspace people run themselves. [Current positioning](https://ii.inc/).\n3. **Seek a warm introduction if you have a genuine mutual contact.** Otherwise, use a concise public message or the [official II contact route](https://ii.inc/contact). Include the clip.\n4. **Ask for a 15-minute guided trial.** Let him or an II engineer choose the task. Have your environment ready so the first interaction produces useful work.\n\nOnce the demo exists, send something like:\n\n> Emad, I’ve been following Zenith and CommonGround. I’m building Papercusp, a self-hosted workspace for coordinating agents with persistent decisions and completion evidence. This 90-second demo shows a task surviving an interrupted session and finishing with a checked result: [link]. Interested in trying one real II task together for 15 minutes? It passes all six meta-acceptance-rubric@5 criteria, with three recommended corrections. The requester, su-3a3a10c1, has a threaded reply, and I closed their older review request, which was still marked unanswered.\n\nResults per meta criterion:\n\n| Meta criterion | Verdict | Basis |\n|---|---|---|\n| Outcomes, not diff restatements | Pass | All 18 criteria are observable outcomes (benchmark numbers, failure-path behavior, state after an operation, a docs search). R-9 is borderline: it describes a guard test, though its falsifier still makes it a real gate. |\n| Traces to goal or Decision | Pass | Every criterion cites a \n[body omitted]\nd \"BROKEN if\" conditions with concrete numbers or failure states. None relies on adjectives alone. |\n| Failure-mode coverage | Pass, one gap | Covers missing preload, the kill-switch, write load, bloat, retirement without a soak, websearch operators, the exact-list cap, and invalid or seed-chased runs. D-023's stale-state guard on `bm25_get_current_score` is not covered. |\n| Non-overlapping | Pass, one note | One recall loss fails both R-13 and R-18. It can now also set a \"nothing outside this list\" ceiling, but it still can't grant anything.\n- **Role\n[body omitted]\n check as well.\n\nThe other claims I checked were accurate, including \"nine pre-write hooks\" and the mail, budget, ledger and default-deny answers.\n\n**How it's now tied to Papercusp:**\n- **Layer map:** a new table after G0 lists each guardrail layer, what it does, and how much of it is really switched on today.\n- **Existing answers:** G0, G1, G2, G3, G6, G7, E1 and E3 now use real examples:\n  - Removing a tool on the client side only hides it from the model; it's still reachable through `tools:invoke`.\n  - The edit hooks run before every file edit.\n  - Each prompt is stamped as typed by you or injected by a machine.\n  - A bug can't be closed as done without evidence for its root cause.\n  - An acceptance grade is refused if the grader descends from the agent that did the work.\n\n**New agent-identity questions (Part 3c):**\n- **I1:** how a tool knows which agent is calling, and why it can't just say its name.\n- **I2:** what stops a stale, resumed or copied session from keeping authority it shouldn't have.\n- **I3:** how to tell which agent did something, and whether you actually asked for it.\n- **I4:** how an agent is stopped from grading its own work.\n\n**Things to know:**\n- I3's \"real example\" is today's pause broadcast: the agent found no matching instruction from you, so it asked you directly.\n- I extended the verifier, but I haven't proved it would fail on the old wording.\n- The work item closed as \"proposed\" rather than fully verified, because `~/interview-prep` isn't a git checkout.\n- I filed WI-6554700010009335, assigned to me: a code comment wrongly says the testing bypass leaves role restrictions in place. I couldn't confirm the pause came from Avi himself, but honoring a pause is the safe choice. Nothing is half-written: I hold no locks, claims or loops. The target is still `gradingAudit.state: pending`, so the dispatcher can re-dispatch after resume. I sent su-70c554c7 an acknowledgment, which was accepted.\n\n**State at the stop:** the scorecard was current and pending, and the lease matched the brief (`grading-audit:EI-25801505\n[body omitted]\n8, which passed 71 of 71 with nothing skipped, on a clean tree at commit `3b657afb5c9e1b9914297368f01950863c5ae733`. The case tied to the spec clause is among the passing tests.\n- **Binding 39483:** points to mutation-probe run 21155112 at the same commit. The mutant made 2 tests fail, including the clause's own test case.\n- **Committed files:** `audit.test.ts`, `audit.ts` and `product-shaping.ts` at that commit exactly match the files the bindings pinned.\n- **Clause coverage:** spec clause AUTO-BAR-R-5-P-003, revision 2, says valid evidence must still reach the completion and activation audit writers after a shaping preview reports gaps. The test checks that both writers are called.\n- **Rerun recipe:** I replayed it with its stored arguments through `plans:evaluate-spec-test-adequacy`, which is read-only. It reproduced every rating exactly: 8 pass, 1 not-applicable, overall pass.\n- **Store per criterion:** each criterion cites the right source. The correct-layer criterion explicitly leaves out the mutation binding.\n- **Re-rating:** this card is the latest of three by the same grading pattern on this subject (EI-25540889103683068, EI-25778125194251713, then this one). It was computed at emit time, not copied from an earlier one.\n\n**Ratings I would have emitted:** all 6 grading-integrity criteria pass. The re-run probes work. The one soft spot is that oracle independence and fixture calibration rest on what the author asserted in the bindings, not on a measurement. I looked at the test body, and it compares against fixed expected values rather than ones computed from the code under test.\n\n**After Avi resumes:** the next auditor must re-read the target, confirm it is still current and pending, check the lease, and then emit. I did not emit an audit or any other scorecard.","owner":"su-4802391a-05f4-41b7-b028-a3021e0078aa","turn_idx":6,"session_id":"665a50ff-03b7-41c4-966e-834e77aa20b8","source_kind":"claude"},{"ts":"2026-10-12T20:50:09.24+00:00","text":"I filed the grading-integrity audit for scorecard EI-25801505495427884. All 6 criteria pass, and the audit was recorded as EI-25808157862178712.\n\n**Resuming:** Avi's resume message carried owner-verified relay provenance (his words: \"TELL ALL AGENTS SYSTEM WIDE TO RESUME THEIR WORK\"). Before emitting I re-read the target. It was still current and pending, its evidence fingerprint was unchanged (`aca9955c…`), and the reservation was still my original lease (`grading-audit:EI-25801505495427884`, reserved 2026-10-12T19:32:15.618Z). No renewal was involved. I did not re-read the target after the emit to confirm it now shows as settled.\n\n**Ratings:**\n- **base-rate-check — pass:** the target has no downgrades. Every pass rests on something I confirmed: test run 21155058 passed 71 of 71 at commit `3b657afb5c9e1b9914297368f01950863c5ae733`. Mutation run 21155112 failed on the spec clause's own test case, so the test catches a broken version. `disclosed-gap` is rated not-applicable only after both bindings were checked, not because nothing was said.\n- **instrument-split — pass:** each criterion reads the right source. Execution comes from test-run records, freshness from file fingerprints, and correct-layer explicitly leaves out the mutation binding. The pinned blobs match the files at that commit.\n- **terminal-re-rate — pass:** the card was written after both runs finished, and its fingerprint differs from the two earlier cards on the same subject. Replaying its stored rerun recipe reproduced all 9 ratings exactly.\n- **platform-telemetry-excluded — pass:** no criterion relies on activity or platform telemetry.\n- **re-runnable-evidence — pass:** the recipe is complete, the bindings keep the full commit SHA and file path, and the replay matched. Two small gaps: the recipe stores a shortened evaluator-build SHA, and the evidence reference embeds a shortened commit SHA. The full commit SHA is still in the binding details.\n- **degenerate-distribution — pass:** 8 pass and 1 not-applicable is lopsided, but each pass rests on a separate fact I confirmed. From 19:49 to 21:43 UTC, 4 reports were refused that way and never got reviewed.\n\n**What I changed:**\n- **Migration 1477 is now live on the shared database.** It changes two things:\n  - An item waiting on agent review now counts as naming its blocker.\n  - A blocking reason only counts for 15 minutes after it's written. Before this, a reason left over from an earlier block could wave through a later block that named nothing.\n  \n  Because both changes are in the database, they also cover the older builds still running.\n- **The 4 refused reports are now in agent review,** with their leftover reasons cleared.\n- **I fixed a second code path with the same ordering problem.** It's another place that parks failed items for a fleet leader to sort out. It set \"blocked\" before writing the reason, so the new rule refused it, and it now writes the reason first.\n\n**How I know it works:**\n- The first report filed after the fix (EI-25811909449949923) went into agent review with no refusal.\n- The database log shows only those 4 refusals, all from before the fix.\n- The new database test (10 tests) and the updated tests for that code path (42) pass, as do the original rule's 8. The typecheck is clean.\n\n**Still open (tracked in WI-6554700010009273):**\n- **The code path fix isn't running anywhere yet,** including :3070, which won't get it until the release check goes green. Until then, if that path tries to block an item, the database refuses it and the item stays open instead. That hasn't happened yet.\n- **7 blocked items with no named blocker belong to other machines,** and can only be fixed there.\n- **The check that's meant to catch risky database changes before they go live doesn't look for new rules that refuse writes,** which is how this slipped through. release plans is still The code rejects that pairing (`packages/operator-core/lib/rubrics.ts:4905–4913`), and lines 4899–4904 say a layer the run ledger never records can never pass. Their proof is CI runs in papercup-rust-mobile, and this harness's ledger doesn't record those. So the declared layers could never be satisfied, and 1177440 itself would have been rejected because it changes R-17.\n- **Nothing else in the three bars changed.** The plan's revision-31 requirements and the requester's amendment script both show the same condition, falsifier and method. The method still names the XCUITest, Compose UI, cargo and unit runs and the CI run IDs the grader must cite.\n- **R-7 is untouched.** It still requires the iOS and Android test suites to exist and pass.\n- **Precedent:** R-6 and R-8 are already manual bars with no test layers.\n\n**One gap that remains:** after the change, R-16's tests for the cache's locked and unpaired states are required only by its method text. R-7's list of tested states doesn't include them. That's a reliance on the grader following the method rather than a weakening, so it didn't block approval.\n\n**Not checked:** I still couldn't confirm that the new hashes match the new text, because this session has no tool that can compute a hash. The apply guard re-checks them.\n\nI sent the requester (`su-440f5011…`) the approval reference and approver ID to use when applying. The wake was queued, but pickup isn't confirmed yet.\n\nThe dropped connection (`read ECONNRESET`) happened a second time.
